                             O R D E R
Heard learned Senior Counsel/Counsel for the parties.
2. A Status Report dated 03.08.2026 has been submitted
on behalf of the Central Bureau of Investigation (CBI) by
Mr. Saurabh Gupta, IPS, Superintendent of Police, CBI,
who was entrusted with conducting an exercise in terms of
the order of this Court dated 21.07.2026.
3. The report called for was essentially a fact-finding
exercise. As per the report, the focus of the exercise
was to ascertain whether a 24-metre-wide road, which cuts
across the DLF Primus Gurugram project, was in fact being
maintained as a road or whether its nature had been
5

## Page 13

CIVIL APPEAL  NO. 8049/2023
altered in any manner. From the material placed before us
including the maps and photographs, there is no dispute
that the 24-metre-wide road, which was to cut across the
entire  project,  does  not  exist  as  such  within  the
project, and portions thereof are being used either for
parking or have been developed as a green track. 
4. We  pause  here  to  indicate  that  from  the  very
beginning, this Court has been emphasizing that the
project, as sold to the consumers, was required to be
delivered/constructed strictly in accordance with the
representations made in the brochure/plan provided by DLF
to the prospective buyers/consumers. A comparison of the
same with the report and photographs contained in the
Status Report dated 03.08.2026 leaves no manner of doubt
that the 24-metre-wide road in the middle of the project
is not in conformity with the original plan/brochure. The
deviation is not minor but substantial, inasmuch as, out
of the 147-metre stretch earmarked for the 24-metre-wide
road, a stretch of about 52 metres has been developed as
a green patch, while another substantial area, almost of
an equivalent extent, as appears from the photographs,
though  retaining  the  appearance  of  a  plain  road
structure, is being used for parking by the residents and
visitors.  Thus,  out  of  the  said  147-metre  stretch,
approximately 100 metres is either a green patch or is
6

## Page 14

CIVIL APPEAL  NO. 8049/2023
being used for parking, constituting almost two-thirds of
the entire stretch.
5. We are at a loss to understand why, despite repeated
opportunities having been granted to ensure that the
project  is  brought  into  conformity  with  what  was
represented in the brochure, the aforesaid deviation has
not yet been rectified. 
6. The stand of the Fire Department, as reflected in the
report  submitted  on  its  behalf,  indicates  that  the
project satisfies the prescribed fire safety standards. 
7. We have also heard one of the respondents, who joined
the proceedings in person through virtual mode. 
8. Mr. Saurabh Gupta, IPS, Superintendent of Police,
CBI, draws our attention to the previous order dated
21.07.2026, by which he was granted two months’ time to
submit a report after conducting a Preliminary Enquiry
(PE). He further submits that the conduct of the PE would
be facilitated if additional manpower is made available
to him for its expeditious completion.
9. Having considered the matter in its entirety, and
keeping in view that the CBI has already been granted
time to complete the PE and submit its report, we are
inclined to adjourn the matter to await the final report.
The Director, CBI, is accordingly requested to assign two
additional Inspectors to assist Mr. Saurabh Gupta in
7

## Page 15

CIVIL APPEAL  NO. 8049/2023
completing the PE.  
10.  We are also not satisfied with the conduct of the
State of Haryana and its officials in the matter, both
with regard to the acquisition of the approximately 100-
metre portion of land required for the 24-metre-wide road
to connect the project with the 60-metre Sector Road, and
with regard to the failure to take effective steps to
overcome the impediments and bring to an end any pending
litigation that may be coming in the way of holding
elections to the Residents Welfare Association. 
11. Accordingly, taking a holistic view of the entire
matter and by way of an extraordinary indulgence, the
matters be listed on 12.10.2026 at 3.00 p.m.
12. The Status Report dated 03.08.2026 filed on behalf of
the CBI shall be supplied to the parties. 
13. It is made clear that if by the next date of hearing
the entire project is not completed in all respects in
accordance with the representations made in the brochure,
this Court shall proceed to pass appropriate orders. The
State and its officials are also cautioned that if by the
next date, necessary steps in all respects are not taken
by  them,  this  Court  shall  not  show  any  further
indulgence. 
14. In the meantime, Mr. Lokesh Sinhal, learned Senior
Additional Advocate General for the State of Haryana, as
8

## Page 16

CIVIL APPEAL  NO. 8049/2023
also  the  concerned  respondents,  shall  file  detailed
responses  to  the  affidavits  filed  on  behalf  of  the
private parties. Such responses shall also disclose the
steps taken generally to ensure that all constructions
are henceforth carried out strictly in accordance with
the provisions of the relevant statutes.
15. Before concluding, we clarify that the CBI shall not
be influenced or prejudiced by anything that may have
transpired, or by any observations made, during the
course of these proceedings. Any such observations were
only intended to sensitize the parties to the seriousness
of the issues involved. The CBI shall, therefore, conduct
the PE objectively and independently.  
       (POOJA SHARMA)                         (ANJALI PANWAR)
          AR-CUM-PS                        ASSISTANT  REGISTRAR

                             O R D E R
Today, though prayer for adjournment has been made on behalf
of respondent no.12-Delhi Golf Club due to personal reason of
learned senior counsel representing it, while accommodating such

## Page 20

4
request, we would require the respondent no.12-Delhi Golf Club to
bring on record the details with regard to what are the relevant
Rules/Byelaws applicable and the modalities/procedures/practices
adopted/followed relating to grant of membership to the Club for
all categories.
2. Mr. Gopal Sankaranarayanan, learned Court Commissioner has
filed a report pursuant to his inspection held on 31.07.2026. As
has been pointed out by the learned Court Commissioner, by and
large things seem to have moved in the right direction. However,
the main concern which is flagged is that there is a lack of
coordination between the Contractors, who are to complete the
horticulture work and who is supposed to do the civil work. In the
said report, the photographs enclosed also indicate that there is a
mismatch in the way the work is required to be completed and what
is actually on ground.
3. Having regard to the aforesaid, we direct that the two
Contractors, who have been allotted the work concerned, shall not
only coordinate among themselves but also take instructions and be
guided by the learned Court Commissioner in matters where there
appears to be any sort of overlapping or there may be lack of
clarity in the matter. The said Contractors would ultimately carry
out  the  work  as  will  be  suggested  by  the  learned  Court
Commissioner.
4. Mr. Shikhil Suri, learned Senior Counsel points out that with
regard to the construction of toilet, the structure appears to be
of a permanent nature, which ought not to be permitted as per the

## Page 21

5
provisions of the relevant Statutes also. On this, as we have been
informed that the toilets, which are presently constructed, serve
the public interest for the reason that it takes care of sewerage,
water and other issues connected with toilets and keeps the issues
of sanitation etc. under check and control rather than an  ad-
hoc/semi-permanent structure. Thus, for the present, we are not
disturbing the arrangement. However, the Department of Archeology
is directed to ensure proper upkeep and cleanliness of the said
toilet complex. 
5. At this juncture, attention has been drawn to the application
filed  on  behalf  of  the  petitioner  seeking  directions  (I.A.
No.254096 of 2026). From the materials brought on record in the
affidavit, it is clear that there has been total callousness and
rather suppression of facts on behalf of the MCD inasmuch as, the
pictures reveal that with regard to certain structures which were
referred to in the affidavit filed on behalf of the MCD on
10.04.2026 a categorical stand was taken that the said structures
were  free  of  encroachment  and  in  a  satisfactory  state  of
conservation. The photographs which have been printed in the Daily
Hindustan Times Newspaper dated 10.08.2026 and in the Daily Times
of India Newspaper, which is probably dated 26.07.2026 indicate a
completely different picture to the stand taken in the affidavit
filed by the MCD. We are unable to understand as to how such stand
could be taken before this Court by an organisation like the MCD in
the present proceedings.
6. There appears to be similar conduct on the part of the ASI

## Page 22

6
with regard to Atgah Khan’s Tomb, Gogabai’s Tomb and Lal Chaubara,
where the stand in the affidavit filed on its behalf on 07.04.2026
is belied by the photographs brought on record as published/printed
in the aforesaid two Daily Newspapers.
7. Having regard to the aforesaid, the Court has no option but to
direct for the personal appearance of the Additional Commissioner,
South Delhi, Municipal Corporation of Delhi as also the Director
General of ASI to show-cause, by filing their personal affidavit,
as to why, the Court should not take serious note of such conduct
and pass appropriate orders. The said reply to the show-cause shall
be filed after fresh site/ground inspection and shall be affirmed
by the Additional Commissioner of New Delhi Municipal Corporation
and the Director General of ASI. The Head of the Archaeological
Department, Government of NCT of Delhi shall also file a personally
affirmed affidavit after a fresh site/ground inspection. It goes
without saying that such inspection(s) shall be carried out in the
presence of the learned Court Commissioner or any member(s) of his
team.
8. The Court was also of the view that some strict orders be
passed against the said two concerned officials of the MCD and the
ASI right away. However, on being persuaded by Mr. S.D. Sanjay,
learned Senior Counsel appearing on behalf of the MCD and Ms.
Aishwarya Bhati, learned Additional Solicitor General appearing on
behalf of ASI, we refrain from doing so, for the present.
9. We make it clear that if the situation continues with regard
to all the structures relating to which the MCD/ASI/Archaeological

## Page 23

7
Department, Government of NCT of Delhi have purportedly conducted
surveys and given a report by way of an affidavit before this Court
and the faxts stated therein are found to be incorrect, straight-
away, the Court may take direct action against the concerned
officials without giving any further indulgence.
10. As we find that it is a herculean task for the learned Court
Commissioner and his team to individually go and visit all the
spots, we request him to get in touch with two journalists, whose
reports are published in the aforesaid two Daily Newspapers i.e.,
Hindustan Times and Times of India and request them to give details
with regard to any further information connected with the present
case available with them and also if they could consult their
colleagues whether they may be having any such information. We
appreciate  the  articles  of  the  said  two  journalists  namely
Mr. Paras Singh associated with the Hindustan Times and Ms. Richi
Verma associated with the Times of India for bringing into the
public domain such disturbing ground reality.
11. We indicate that it is primarily the duty of the State
Archaeological Department and the Delhi Tourism and Transportation
Development Corporation (DTTDC) to ensure that the work is carried
out smoothly with regard to  Gumti of Shaikh Ali.  If this Court
finds that they are not properly monitoring the coordination of the
work, the Court may go into the aspect of allottment/finalisation
of  contracts  by  these  two  organisations  and  also  fix
accountability, both individual and organisational, which may not
be restricted to the present transaction only.

## Page 24

8
12. It appears that though this Court vide its earlier order dated
22.07.2026  had  made  a  correction  with  regard  to  the
school/institution where Kharbooze ka Gumbad was located, we are
informed that the school in question would be  Vidya Bal Vihar
instead  of  Panchshila  Montessori  School,  Sadhna  Enclave,
functioning on land leased from the Panchsheel Cooperative House
Building Society Limited. Accordingly, the name of respondent no.13
be substituted as Vidya Bal Vihar. Let fresh notice be issued to
the said respondent no.13. The ASI shall furnish a fresh report
with regard to the Kharbooze ka Gumbad in the newly added school in
question-respondent no.13 as also the Lal Gumbad, which is in close
proximity thereof. It shall be the duty of the respondents to
verify from the records as to who is responsible for their upkeep
and  the  said  agency/party  shall  file  its  reply.  For  ready
reference, the report of the learned Court Commissioner dated
05.08.2026 and the materials/photographs appended thereto can be a
starting point.
13. All parties to file a revised factual report based on actual
site inspection, which shall be conducted afresh, in the presence
of the learned Court Commissioner or any member(s) of his team. Any
lack of information on facts or suppression or incorrect facts
being reported, shall entail serious consequences.
14. With regard to Sunder Nursery, whatever area has been settled
with the  Aga Khan Trust , the entire details and terms of such
settlement, including, inter alia, the audit reports of the said
Trust with regard to how the finances are generated and utilised,

9
payment of salary to the staff are made etc., a detailed affidavit
shall be filed by the Authority concerned.

We have heard the learned counsel appearing for
the appellants and the learned counsel appearing for
some of the respondents. Despite notice served, there
in  representation  on  behalf  of  most  of  the

judgment of this Court in Civil Appeal Nos.909-910 of
2026, titled as ‘ The Deputy Commissioner and Special
Land Acquisition Officer v. M/s S.V. Global Mill
Limited’ decided on 09.02.2026.

set aside the impugned order(s) passed by the High
Court in terms of the aforesaid judgment.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION 
CRIMINAL APPEAL NO.       /2026
[@ SPECIAL LEAVE PETITION (Crl.)  No. 8590/2026]
TAYYAB                           APPELLANT(S)
                   VERSUS
STATE OF U.P.                    RESPONDENT(S)
                  O R D E R
1. Leave granted.
2. Heard learned counsel for the parties.
3. This appeal arises from an order of the
High Court of judicature at Allahabad dated
09.04.2026 whereby the regular bail prayer of
the appellant in connection with Case Crime
No.170 of 2025 registered at P.S. Jhinjhana,
District Shamli was rejected.
4. The submission on behalf of the appellant
is that, though the appellant is husband of the
deceased, the facts would indicate that deceased
1

## Page 34

Crl.A. @ SLP (Crl.) No.8590/2026
consumed poison and was brought to the hospital
by her father-in-law and brother-in-law where
she died later. No external injuries were found
on her body and, therefore, it may be a case of
suicide. FIR was lodged thereafter implicating
the appellant as well as all relatives of the
appellant  by  making  false  allegations  of
harassment on account of dowry demand. It has
been submitted that except the appellant, rest
of the accused have been admitted to bail.  It
is submitted that investigation is complete;
appellant has no criminal antecedents; and by
now he has suffered incarceration for a period
exceeding one year.  In such circumstances, it
has been prayed that the appellant be released
on bail.
5. The  learned  counsel  for  the  State  has
opposed the prayer for bail but could not refute
that no ante mortem injuries on the body of the
2

## Page 35

Crl.A. @ SLP (Crl.) No.8590/2026
deceased were noticed during autopsy.
6. Having  regard  to  the  submissions  made
before us, without expressing any opinion on the
merits of the prosecution case, we are of the
view  that  the  appellant  is  entitled  to  be
released on bail at this stage, pending trial.
7. The appeal is, accordingly, allowed. The
impugned order of the High Court rejecting the
bail prayer of the appellant is set aside. 
8. The appellant shall be released on bail on
such terms and conditions as the trial court may
deem  fit  to  impose  in  the  facts  and
circumstances of the case.
9. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand
disposed of.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
CRIMINAL APPEAL No.4069 OF 2026
[arising out of SLP (Crl.) No.12867/2026]
PAPPU KUMAR & ANR.       APPELLANTS
VERSUS
THE STATE OF BIHAR      RESPONDENT
 
O R D E R 
1. Leave granted.
2. The  High  Court  of  Punjab  and  Haryana  of
Judicature at Patna, by the impugned judgment and
order  dated  01 st July,  2026,  has  rejected  the
appellants’ prayer for bail in anticipation of arrest. 
3. Appellants, Pappu Kumar and Vishundev Thakur,
1

## Page 40

figure as accused in FIR No.277 of 2025 dated 04 th
October, 2025 registered at Police Station Rajeipur
Thana, District Motihari, under Sections 190, 191(2),
191(3), 126(2), 115(2), 109, 351(2), 303(2) and 352
of the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS), 2023.
4. We have heard learned counsel appearing for
the parties.
5. It is not disputed by learned counsel for the
respondent-State of Bihar that the appellants have
joined investigation in terms of the earlier order of
this Court.
6. Having regard to the facts and circumstances
and  the  nature  of  allegations,  we  are  of  the
considered opinion that the appellants’ detention for
custodial interrogation is not necessary and that they
may be admitted to an order for grant of bail in
anticipation of arrest.
7. We, accordingly, set aside the judgment and
order impugned in this appeal.
2

## Page 41

8. It is directed that in the event of the appellants
being arrested, they shall be released on bail on
terms and conditions to be imposed by the trial court.
9. Since the investigation is yet to conclude, we
direct that if the investigating officer calls upon the
appellants to continue to join the investigation, they
shall do so by attending the police station failing
which liberty is granted to bring any such omission to
the notice of the trial court and seek cancellation of
bail.
10. Needless to observe, the appellants shall not,
directly or indirectly, by making inducement, threat
or promise, dissuade any person acquainted with the
facts of the case from disclosing such facts to any
police officer or to the court.
11. We clarify that the observations made in this
order  and  grant  of  bail  to  the  appellants  in
anticipation of arrest will not be treated as findings
on the merits of the case.
3

## Page 42

12. The  appeal  is,  accordingly,  allowed  on  the
aforesaid terms.
13. Pending application(s), if any, stand disposed of.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
CIVIL APPEAL No.11960/2026
@ SLP (C) No.11918/2026
J. M. AMANULLAH APPELLANT 
VERSUS
   M/S PRANAV FOUNDATION PRIVATE 
    LIMITED & ANR.      RESPONDENTS
O R D E R
1. Leave granted.
2. The appeal is directed against the order dated 05 th
March,  2026  passed  by  the  High  Court  of  Judicature  at
Madras on a writ petition1 filed by the respondent no.1. 
3.  While  disposing  of  the  writ  petition,  the  High  Court
directed as follows:-
“7. In order to test the  bona  fide on the part of the
petitioner as  he is  a  borrower  and  the  owner of  the
property in question, we deem it appropriate to pass the
following orders:
(i)  that  the  writ  petitioner  can  approach  the  Debts
Recovery Tribunal by filing a memo to this effect along
with  a  copy  of  this  order  in  the  pending  SARFAESI
Application in S.A.No.83 of 2026 immediately where the
1  WP No. 8810/2026
1

## Page 62

intention of the petitioner can very well be stated to the
Debts  Recovery  Tribunal  that  the  petitioner  would
deposit Rs.25 Crores within a period of four weeks from
the date of receipt of a copy of this order.
(ii) On receipt of such memo to be filed in this regard by
the petitioner along with the copy of this order, it is open
to  the  Debts  Recovery  Tribunal-Ill,  Chennai  to  decide
LA.No.291  of  2026  immediately  not  waiting  till  the
further date of hearing that has already been slated to
be on 28.04.2026 after hearing the parties concerned,
i.e., Bank as well as auction purchaser on merits and in
accordance with law.
(iii)  Till  such  time,  i.e.,  within  thirty  days  period  as
indicated hereinabove, no further encumbrance be made
by the second respondent in respect of the properly in
question.
(iv) Beyond thirty days period, if the petitioner is not
complied  with the  assurance  that  has  been indicated
hereinabove as recorded in this order within thirty days
time or if any further order on merits is passed by the
Debts Recovery Tribunal as directed above, it is open to
the second respondent / auction purchaser to meddle
with the property in the manner known to law as per the
right accrued on him.”
4. We  are  informed  that  the  respondent  no.1  had
approached the Debts Recovery Tribunal 2, Chennai under
Section  17  of  the  Securitisation  and  Reconstruction  of
Financial Assets and Enforcement of Security Interest Act,
20023 and that such application has since been dismissed on
09th July, 2026.
2  DRT
3  SARFAESI Act
2

## Page 63

5. Mr. M. P. Parthiban, learned counsel appearing for
the respondent no.1, submits that an appeal under Section
18  of  the  SARFAESI  Act has  been  carried  to  the  Debts
Recovery Appellate Tribunal4, Chennai. 
6. Since the application  of the respondent no.1  has
failed  before  the  DRT  and  appeal  before  the  DRAT  is
pending, we set aside the impugned judgment and order.
7. All points are, however, kept open on merits for the
respondent no.1 and the petitioner as well as the secured
creditor to urge before the DRAT.

the Mediation Centre of this Court to explore
possibility of a settlement.
3. Pursuant  thereto,  the  Supreme  Court  Mediation
Centre  has  forwarded  a  Settlement  Deed  dated
06.08.2026, duly signed by the petitioner and her
husband (the second respondent), Pavan Kumar Gaur,
incorporating the terms of the settlement arrived
through mediation. 
1

## Page 67

4. Under the settlement, the parties have agreed to a

respondent (the husband) has agreed to pay Rs.50
lakhs to the petitioner-wife by way of full and
final settlement of her claims out of which Rs.30
lakhs has been paid at the time of entering the
settlement and Rs.20 lakhs have been credited to
the account of Sonam Yadav (petitioner) by RTGS
transfer dated 24.08.2026. Sonam Yadav (i.e., the
petitioner) has also appeared in person through
video conference, and she admits that the balance
amount of Rs.20 lakhs have been transferred to her
account.  
5. Terms  of  the  settlement  also  requires
quashing/withdrawal of all proceedings  inter  se
parties which are described in paragraph (n) of the
Settlement Agreement.  These are:
(i) Maintenance Case bearing Case No.E/135/2026,
“Sonam vs. Pavan” pending in the Court of Family
Judge, Pune, Maharashtra;
(ii)   Maintenance  Case  bearing  No.E/134/2026,
“Vivan vs. Pavan” pending in the Court of Family
Judge, Pune, Maharashtra.
(iii)  Divorce Case No.A/1340/2026 pending in he
2

Court of Family Judge, Pune, Maharashtra; and
(iv) Case No.3326/2025 pending in the Court of
Civil  Judge  (J.D.),  FTC,(CAW),  Jhasi,  U.P.
emanating from FIR No.030/2024 and five domestic
Violence Case No.798/2024 pending before the Court
at Pune.
6. The learned counsel for the parties as well as the
parties  present  in-person  through  video
conferencing pray that a decree of dissolution of
marriage  by  mutual  consent  be  passed  besides
quashing/withdrawal  of  all  proceedings  pending
inter se parties.
7. The learned counsel for the parties also pray that
this Court may invoke its powers under Article 142
of the Constitution of India and put a quietus to
various litigations between the parties by quashing
those proceedings.  
8. Having regard to the settlement between the parties
as also that under the terms and conditions of the
settlement, the second respondent has already made
payment of Rs.50 lakhs to the petitioner, we deem
it appropriate to invoke our powers under Article
142 of the Constitution of India for doing complete
justice in the matter and put a quietus to various

disputes/ litigations between the parties.

proceedings inter se parties mentioned in paragraph
(n)  of  the  settlement  agreement  shall  stand
quashed/withdrawn in terms of the settlement.

settlement. The decree of dissolution of marriage
shall enclose copy of the settlement.

disposed of.

## Page 76

Page 1 of 4 
 
 
IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA 
CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION 
 
Criminal Appeal No.                 of 2026 
[ @ Special Leave Petition (Crl) No.12169 of 2026 ] 
 
 
Anil Singh @ Anil Kumar Singh   ...Appellant 
 
 
 
Versus 
 
 
The State of Bihar & Ors.          ...Respondents 
 
 
O R D E R 
Leave granted. 
2. The question arising in the above case is as to the 
sustainability of an application under Section 311 of the Code 
of Criminal Procedure, 1973 1 to examine  certain witnesses 
after the examination of the accused under Section 313 CrPC 
had commenced on 13.04.2024, in a trial, which commenced 
in the year 2017. The Additional District and Sessions Judge-
II Gopal ganj, where the trial was pending , rejected the 
application which was confirmed by the impugned order of 
the High Court. The complainant an injured witness , is the 
 
1 For brevity, ‘the Cr.P.C.’ 

## Page 77

Page 2 of 4 
 
 
appellant here; he having filed the application under Section 
311 before the Sessions Court. 
3. On the brief facts, it is to be noticed that there was an 
altercation between the accused and the injured , wherein 
accused No.1 was accused of attacking the appellant’s father 
with a spear, accused Nos.3 & 4 of an assault with an iron rod 
and accused No.2 having struck the appellant on the head 
with a sword. After investigation , charge was laid only 
against A1 and A2 that against A3 and A4 , both women, 
having been dropped. Cognizance too was taken only 
against A1 and A2  and the trial proceeded and concluded , 
when the application under Section 311 was filed. Both the 
Trial Court and the High Court found fault , on the premise 
that it was a dilatory tactic and the attempt was to plug the 
lacunae in the testimony already recorded. 
4. Normally, we would not have entertained the 
application under Article 136 of the Constitution of India, 
however, in the present case , what is projected is a clear 
failure of the prosecution to properly conduct the trial. The 
appellant and his father were injured in the incident. The 
father was not examined and the other witnesses sought to be 
examined are the Doctor, who attended to the injured and the 

## Page 78

Page 3 of 4 
 
 
Investigating Officer; crucial witnesses . The absence of 
whom could lead to even an acquittal for reason of the 
shoddy prosecution carried out  on the allegation of lacunae 
being attempted to be plugged; it is the prosecution that 
proffers witnesses in a trial and here, the failure is pointed 
out by the de-facto complainant. 
5. We are unable to sustain the orders passed and set 
aside Annexure P4 order of the Additional District and 
Sessions Judge-II Gopalganj and the impugned order of the 
High Court. The application under Section 311  shall stand 
restored before the Trial Court and the prayer for 
examination of the injured witness, the medical officer and 
the Investigating Officer shall definitely be permitted. If any 
other witnesses are sought to be examined, which the 
prosecution has left out, as is the case of the injured witness 
and the medical officer, the Trial Court shall bestow its 
consideration to such request  too. The witnesses shall be 
examined and proffered for cross -examination. Section 313 
shall be commenced from the stage, it was stopped, ensuring 
that any in criminating circumstance brought out in the 
testimony of the witnesses now examined are also put to the 
accused. 

## Page 79

Page 4 of 4 
 
 
6. We cannot but express a note of caution, that when 
there is total failure of the prosecution, the Court should with 
alacrity question the prosecution’s conduct in not examining 
crucial witnesses. The Trial Court is not a moot spectator and 
even in the r ole of an unbiased adjudicator has the 
responsibility to ensure that the best evidence is not 
suppressed by the prosecution, as in this case, where even 
the Medical Officer and the Investigating Officer was not 
examined. 
7. The appeal stands allowed with the above directions. 
8. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed of. 
 
 
.......…….……………………. J. 
                (J. B. PARDIWALA) 
 
 
....……….……………………. J. 
                            (K. VINOD CHANDRAN) 
 
NEW DELHI; 
AUGUST 24, 2026.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL ORIGINAL JURISDICTION
TRANSFER PETITION (CIVIL) NO.2056/2025
AYUSHI YADAV                                   PETITIONER(s)
                               VERSUS
DEEPENDRA SINGH KOSHIYARI                      RESPONDENT(s)
O R D E R
1. By our order dated 06 th May, 2026, we had appointed Ms.
Shirin Khajuria, the learned senior advocate and senior
trained mediator to act as a Mediator to try to bring
around some settlement between the parties.
2. We are informed that Ms. Khajuria tried her level best to
persuade  the  parties  to  reach  to  some  amicable  and
equitable  settlement,  however,  unfortunately,  the
mediation has failed.
3. The matter was taken up for further hearing today in the
Committee Room.
4. The parties are personally present with their respective
counsel. The parents of Deependra are also personally
present.
5. We once again tried to persuade Ayushi to consider the
proposal which has been put forward on behalf of Deependra
and put an end to the entire dispute. However, Ayushi is
not agreeable to any of the proposals.
1

## Page 102

6. In such circumstances, we are left with no other option
but to close this matter.
7. Transfer, as prayed for is allowed.
8. Consequently, the Original Suit No.710/2025 titled as
“Deependra Singh Koshiyari vs. Ayushi Yadav”, pending
before the Court of Additional Principal Judge-1, Family
Court Dehradun, Uttarakhand is ordered to be transferred
to the Family Court, Saket District Court, South District,
Delhi.
9. The transferor court shall forthwith transmit the record
of the aforesaid case to the transferee court.
10. The transferee court shall make all possible endeavours
first for an amicable settlement between the parties,
failing which for an early decision in the matter without
unnecessary adjournments.
11. The respondent-husband is permitted to attend all the
proceedings before the transferee court online, except on
such date(s) when his personal presence is mandatorily
required by the Court.
12. The proceedings between the parties, civil and criminal
shall now proceed further in accordance with law.
13. We express our gratitude towards Ms. Shirin Khajuria for
taking lot of pains in trying to persuade the parties to
reach to some settlement. Unfortunately, the parties have
not been able to reach to any settlement.
2

## Page 103

14. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed of.

                        [J.B. PARDIWALA]
 …………………………………………….J.
 [K.V. VISWANATHAN]
NEW DELHI;
25th AUGUST, 2026

under the Guardians and Wards Act, 1890, we are not

## Page 121

                   
Upon hearing the counsel, the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. We have heard the learned Senior Counsel appearing
for the petitioner and the learned counsel appearing for
the respondents.
2. It is the say of the learned Senior Counsel appearing
for the petitioner that the mentioning made before the
High Court for urgent circulation of the Writ Petition
was declined on the premise that the petitioner has got
an alternative remedy.
3. In our considered view, the High Court ought to have
taken up the subject Writ Petition and thereafter, passed
an order one way or the other, deciding the issues
including  the  issue  of  availability  of  alternative
remedy.
4. Suffice it is to state that the Writ Petition which
was filed by the petitioner, could not have been declined
to be heard by the High Court at mentioning stage itself.
5. In view of the above, we are inclined to dispose of
the Special Leave Petition with a request to the High
Court to take up the Writ Petition and thereafter, take a
decision within a period of one week.
6. We also make it clear that all the issues are left
open including the issue of maintainability and the
2

## Page 122

alternative remedy.
7. In the meantime,  status  quo  existing as on today
shall be maintained by the parties.
8. Accordingly,  the  Special  Leave  Petition  stands
disposed of in the above terms.
9. Pending application(s), if any, shall also stand
disposed of.
 (SHUBHAM SONI)                                  (POONAM VAID)

O R D E R 
 
 
 Heard Ms. Madhavi Divan, learned Senior Counsel for the 
petitioners, Mr. Tushar Mehta, learned Solicitor General for the 
respondent No.1 and Mr. Shoeb Alam for the respondent No.7. 
2. We have considered the matter in depth , we are convinced that 
what has been recorded in its order dated 16.04.2025 has to be 
implemented. Accordingly, the following directions are issued. 
(i)  The respondent No.1, shall , with immediate effect,  ensure 
that the entire building which was damaged in fire is  not used 

## Page 134

3 
SLP (C) No. 7826-7828/2021 
 
 
in any manner whatsoever till it is  fully repaired , in all 
aspects, by the builder. Only upon the same being done, the 
respondent No.1 shall issue the necessary permission for 
use/occupation of the building. 
(ii) Till the time the respondent No.1 is not satisfied that 
the entire building, including all the floors are  fully safe 
for all activities and in a condition which passes the test of 
the statutory requirements, the entire building shall not be 
used for any purpose. 
(iii) The respondent No.1 shall ensure that the building, as a 
whole, does not function and nobody is permitted to enter the 
premises. 
(iv) Only upon the entire construction having been completed, 
and the requisite clearance certificates being issued by the 
respondent No.1, afresh, permission to enter/occupy the 
building shall be granted.  
3. The same be done preferably, within two months from today.  
4. It is clarified that this order does not in any way go into 
the rights and obligations which the parties  may have, in law,  
inter se between themselves. 
5. With the aforesaid direction s, this Special Leave Petition 
stands disposed of. 
6. Pending applications, if any, also stand disposed of.  
 
(SACHIN KUMAR SRIVASTAVA)                       (ANJALI PANWAR) 
    COURT MASTER (SH)                         ASSISTANT REGISTRAR

## Page 139

2. The present proceedings concern eviction of the
petitioner from the premises situated at House bearing
No.8-2-293/82/A/1085  Jubilee  Hills,  Hyderabad.   The
Collector,  the  District  Magistrate,  Hyderabad,  the
Appellate Tribunal, the learned Single Judge and the
Division Bench have all maintained the eviction of the
petitioner under the Maintenance and Welfare of Parents
and  Senior Citizens  Act, 2007  and the  Rules framed
thereunder.
3. Having heard learned senior counsel appearing for
the petitioner, we are not inclined to interfere with the
impugned order.
4. The petitioner shall on or before 31 st December,
2026 hand over vacant and peaceful possession of the
property in question to respondent No.4.  Learned senior
counsel for the petitioner submits that there are civil
proceedings pending between the parties in S.A.No.453 of
2024 before the High Court for the State of Telangana at
Hyderabad and O.S.No.5050 of 2021 pending before the
Court of the Junior Civil Judge, Hyderabad now pending
before  the  X  Additional  Chief  Judge,  Hyderabad  and
renumbered as O.S. No.2 of 2024 as well as O.S.No.151 of
2022 pending before the Court of X Additional Chief
Judge, Hyderabad.
2

## Page 140

5. It is made clear that we have not observed anything
touching upon the proceedings pending before the Civil
Courts and they will be decided on their own merits.
6. We, however, direct that the petitioner shall on or
before 31st August, 2026 file an undertaking in this Court
stating therein that the petitioner will hand over the
premises in question on or before 31 st December, 2026 and
expressly stating that he has not and he will not create
third party rights in the property in question.
7. The Special Leave Petition is disposed of in the
above terms.
8. Pending application, if any, shall stand disposed
of.

## Page 217

1. When the case is called out, learned counsel appearing for the respondent-
Insurance Company has pointed out that the matters has been settled between
the parties. The Settlement Deed has also been placed on record and the thump
impressions has been affixed by the concerned parties. It has also been signed
by Secretary, DLSA.
2. The concerned petitioners have remained present virtually, and we have
personally interacted with them. During the interaction, the petitioners have
stated that they are not satisfied with the amount of compensation being
offered  by  the  respondent-Insurance  Company  and,  therefore,  are  not

                             O R D E R
Heard Mr. C.N. Sreekumar, learned senior counsel for the
petitioner  and  Mr.  Sriram  Parakkat,  learned  counsel  for  the
respondent.
2. Having considered the matter in its entirety and going through
the materials on record, we do not find any infirmity in the
impugned judgment warranting interference by this Court.
3. However, we find that in the facts and circumstances of the
present case, the interest of justice would be served, if the rate
of interest awarded by the High Court is modified. 
4. Accordingly, the rate of interest awarded by the High Court
vide  its impugned judgment dated 05.11.2025 of 6% simple interest
per annum from 23.09.2007 till the date of realisation is reduced
to 3% simple interest per annum.
5. With the aforesaid modification in the said impugned judgment,
the Special Leave Petition stands disposed of.
6. Pending application(s), if any, shall also stand disposed of.

 CONMT.PET.(C) NOS.665-669/2026
IN 
CRIMINAL APPEAL NOS.2513-2517/2026
M S SHIVANGINI PROPERTIES PVT LTD             PETITIONER(S)
                          VERSUS
MR M J SHANTARAM                             RESPONDENT(S)/
ALLEGED CONTEMNOR(S)
O R D E R
1. The  prayer  of  the  respondent  for  suspension  of
sentence was accepted by the Appellate Court subject
to a deposit of 20% of the amount in question.  The
High Court reduced the same to 10% against which the
petitioner filed a criminal appeal which was allowed
vide order dated 14.05.2026.  Consequently, the order
of the High Court reducing the amount to be deposited
was set aside and the order of the Appellate Court was
restored.
2. In such circumstances, if the appellant has not made
the requisite deposit, it is for the Appellate Court
to address the consequences.
3. In our view the contempt petitions are misconceived.
The petitions are, accordingly, dismissed.
4. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed
of.
